Is that... does that make sense why I would come in today?\n\nDoctor: When you're near the front windows or on that part of the street, do you feel anxious or have any physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't think I get sweaty or anything like that. My heart might beat a tiny bit faster, but honestly, it's probably just because I'm moving around the store, you know? It's really nothing serious, I promise. I just... I just feel a little bit uncomfortable, I guess? Like I'd rather just be in the back. Is that... is that an okay answer? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is it only the front windows and that part of the street that you avoid, or do you find yourself avoiding other places or situations as well?\n\nPatient: Um, I think it's mostly just the front of the store and that part of the street. I mean, I guess I've been a little bit more of a homebody lately? But that's just because I've been tired after work, you know? It's not like I'm hiding or anything. I still go to the grocery store if I really need to, as long as it's not too crowded. \n\nI just... I don't want to make a big deal out of it. I'm sure everyone has places they don't really like going, right? I'm trying really hard not to let it affect my life too much. Does that sound like a normal thing to do? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or worried about having to go near the front windows, even when you're not actually there at the moment—like when you're getting ready for work or thinking about your schedule?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really think so? I mean, I guess maybe I think about it a little bit when I'm getting ready, like... I might just plan out how to get from the back room to the break room without having to walk past the front. But it's not like I'm sitting at home panicking or anything! I'm just trying to be organized about my day, you know? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm, like, obsessing over it. It's just a tiny little thought in the morning, and then I go to work and try to do a good job. That's not too weird, is it? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful with my answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any upsetting thoughts or images coming into your mind that you can't seem to get rid of, even if you try not to think about them? And do you ever find yourself doing certain things over and over again to feel better or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't do anything over and over, no. I'm definitely not doing anything like that. But, um... I guess sometimes I do get these thoughts? \n\nIt's really silly, and I try so hard to just push it away, but... sometimes the crash just pops into my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll be trying to set up a display or something, just doing my job, and suddenly it's like I can hear that awful bang again. And... and I can almost see the glass coming down, just for a second, like it's happening right then. \n\nBut I shake it off really fast! I don't let it stop me from working. Just for a split second, though! It's like... I'll be setting up the shelves, and then out of nowhere, it's just there. The sound of the bang is so loud in my head, and I can literally picture the glass shattering and falling, almost like I'm standing right back in that spot when it happened. \n\nBut it really is just a quick thing. I blink, and I'm back in the store, and everything is fine. I don't want you to think I'm completely losing touch with reality or anything like that. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or worried about having to go near the front windows, even when you're not actually there at the moment—like when you're getting ready for work or thinking about your schedule?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really think so? I mean, I guess maybe I think about it a little bit when I'm getting ready, like... I might just plan out how to get from the back room to the break room without having to walk past the front. But it's not like I'm sitting at home panicking or anything! I'm just trying to be organized about my day, you know? \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm, like, obsessing over it. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful with my answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any upsetting thoughts or images coming into your mind that you can't seem to get rid of, even if you try not to think about them? I'm definitely not doing anything like that. But, um... I guess sometimes I do get these thoughts? \n\nIt's really silly, and I try so hard to just push it away, but... sometimes the crash just pops into my head out of nowhere. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, I wouldn't say it's hard to feel happy or anything. I mean, I still love my family and my friends just as much as I always did!

Um... no, I haven't really been thinking things like that. I mean, I know accidents happen, right? It's just a part of life.

I just want to make sure I'm being helpful with my answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any upsetting thoughts or images coming into your mind that you can't seem to get rid of, even if you try not to think about them? And do you ever find yourself doing certain things over and over again to feel better or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't do anything over and over, no. I'm definitely not doing anything like that. But, um... I guess sometimes I do get these thoughts? \n\nIt's really silly, and I try so hard to just push it away, but... sometimes the crash just pops into my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll be trying to set up a display or something, just doing my job, and suddenly it's like I can hear that awful bang again. And... and I can almost see the glass coming down, just for a second, like it's happening right then. \n\nBut I shake it off really fast! I don't let it stop me from working. It's just a quick flash, and then I'm fine. I'm sure it's just my brain being a little tired, right? It's normal to remember things like that sometimes... isn't it? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm losing my mind or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you say the crash pops into your head and you can almost see the glass coming down again, does that feel like you're actually reliving the moment it happened, as if it's happening all over again right then?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it does feel a little bit like that? Just for a split second, though! It's like... I'll be setting up the shelves, and then out of nowhere, it's just there. The sound of the bang is so loud in my head, and I can literally picture the glass shattering and falling, almost like I'm standing right back in that spot when it happened. \n\nBut it really is just a quick thing. I blink, and I'm back in the store, and everything is fine. I don't want you to think I'm completely losing touch with reality or anything like that. It's just a really vivid memory that sneaks up on me. That makes sense, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a strong memory of something scary? I'm trying my best to just work through it when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a little more about the crash you mentioned? Was that something you experienced yourself, or did you see it happen to someone else?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I was there. I experienced it myself. But I wasn't hurt! I really don't want to make it sound like some huge tragedy or anything, I promise. \n\nIt was, um, about two weeks ago... I was working the overnight shift, and a car just crashed right through the store window. So, that's when these little flashes started, just for the last two weeks. But like I said, I was physically totally fine, so I feel kind of silly even making a big deal out of it. It's just a crazy thing to happen on a normal shift, you know? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for... I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Since the car crashed through the window, have you noticed that you've been feeling more negative emotions like fear, sadness, or anger, or that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. I mean, I'm generally a pretty happy person! I try to look on the bright side of things. \n\nI haven't been feeling super sad or angry or anything like that. Maybe just a little... tired? But I think that's just from working a lot, not because of the crash. I'm still doing all my normal stuff, and I still like my job and everything. I'm definitely not letting it get me down. I hope that's okay to say? I just really don't want to come across like I'm complaining or being negative when I don't have a reason to be. I'm doing pretty good, honestly!\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you found yourself avoiding certain thoughts, feelings, or conversations that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I'm avoiding anything on purpose. I mean, I still go to work, obviously, and I still talk to my coworkers. \n\nIf someone brings it up, I'll talk about it... a little bit. I just try not to dwell on it, you know? Like, what's the point of rehashing it over and over? It's better to just move on and focus on the positive stuff. So I guess I don't really think about it too much if I don't have to, but I wouldn't call it avoiding. That's a good way to handle things, right? Just trying to stay upbeat and not let it drag me down. I hope that's the right answer... I'm really trying to be helpful!\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you found yourself being more alert or on edge, like you’re constantly watching for danger, even when you’re in a safe place?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe a little bit? But I think that's pretty normal, right? Like, anyone would be a little jumpy after something like that. \n\nI don't think I'm constantly on edge or anything. I mean, I might look at the window a little more when I'm at work now, just to... check, I guess. But I wouldn't say I'm watching for danger everywhere I go. I'm definitely not paranoid! I still feel totally safe at home and everything. It's just a tiny bit of extra awareness at work, which is probably a smart thing anyway, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. I'm really trying not to overreact to the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days since the crash where you felt unusually upset, scared, or emotional, even when nothing else was happening to cause it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't really had any days like that. I've been keeping pretty steady, honestly. \n\nI mean, I think I'd notice if I was just randomly getting upset for no reason. I try to keep my emotions in check, especially at work, because you have to be cheerful for the customers, you know? I haven't been crying out of nowhere or feeling super scared when I'm just sitting at home. I really try to keep a level head about things. I think I'm handling everything pretty well... at least, I hope I am? I don't want to seem like I'm making a big deal out of nothing. I'm doing okay, really!\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or interest in things you usually enjoy since the crash, like feeling more numb or detached?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I've felt numb or anything like that. I've been trying really hard to keep up with my normal routine. \n\nI mean, I still go out with my friends when they ask, and I still watch my favorite shows. I guess sometimes I might be a little quieter than usual, but I'm definitely not detached! I still care about things and enjoy my life. I just don't want anyone to worry about me, so I make sure to keep a smile on and stay involved. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keeping busy and not letting yourself get down? I really want to make sure I'm bouncing back the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you found it hard to feel positive emotions like happiness or love, even when you're with people you care about or doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say it's hard to feel happy or anything. I mean, I still love my family and my friends just as much as I always did! \n\nI guess... well, maybe I've been a little tired lately, so I might not be, like, bouncing off the walls with excitement all the time. But I definitely still feel happy when I'm with them. I make sure to laugh at their jokes and tell them I love them, because I do! I don't want them to think I'm pulling away or anything. It's totally normal to just be a little drained after an accident, right? I'm still the same person, I promise. I really don't think I've lost the ability to feel good things. That would be awful, wouldn't it?\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you noticed yourself believing things like ‘I’m bad for letting this happen’ or ‘the world isn’t safe anymore’?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't really been thinking things like that. I mean, I know accidents happen, right? It's just a part of life. \n\nI definitely don't think I'm a bad person or anything. I try really hard to focus on the positive and be grateful that it wasn't worse. And as for the world not being safe... well, I still go to work every day and run my errands. I wouldn't do that if I thought everything was completely unsafe, you know? I just try to be a little more careful now, which is just being responsible, isn't it? I really want to make sure I'm having a healthy outlook on everything. I think I'm keeping a pretty good perspective on it all... don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, how many days have you missed work or found it difficult to carry out your usual daily activities because of these thoughts or feelings?\n\nPatient: Um... I really haven't missed any full days of work. The sound of the bang is so loud in my head, and I can literally picture the glass shattering and falling, almost like I'm standing right back in that spot when it happened. \n\nBut it really is just a quick thing. I blink, and I'm back in the store, and everything is fine. I don't want you to think I'm completely losing touch with reality or anything like that. It's just a really vivid memory that sneaks up on me. That makes sense, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a strong memory of something scary? I'm trying my best to just work through it when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a little more about the crash you mentioned? Was that something you experienced yourself, or did you see it happen to someone else?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yeah, I was there. I experienced it myself. But I wasn't hurt! I really don't want to make it sound like some huge tragedy or anything, I promise. \n\nIt was, um, about two weeks ago... I was working the overnight shift, and a car just crashed right through the store window. So, that's when these little flashes started, just for the last two weeks. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Since the car crashed through the window, have you noticed that you've been feeling more negative emotions like fear, sadness, or anger, or that you're not enjoying things as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. I mean, I'm generally a pretty happy person! I try to look on the bright side of things. \n\nI haven't been feeling super sad or angry or anything like that. Maybe just a little... tired? But I think that's just from working a lot, not because of the crash. I'm still doing all my normal stuff, and I still like my job and everything. I'm definitely not letting it get me down. I hope that's okay to say? I just really don't want to come across like I'm complaining or being negative when I don't have a reason to be. I'm doing pretty good, honestly!\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you found yourself avoiding certain thoughts, feelings, or conversations that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I'm avoiding anything on purpose. I mean, I still go to work, obviously, and I still talk to my coworkers. \n\nIf someone brings it up, I'll talk about it... a little bit. I just try not to dwell on it, you know? Like, what's the point of rehashing it over and over? It's better to just move on and focus on the positive stuff. Just trying to stay upbeat and not let it drag me down. I hope that's the right answer... I'm really trying to be helpful!\n\nDoctor: Since the crash, have you found yourself being more alert or on edge, like you’re constantly watching for danger, even when you’re in a safe place?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe a little bit? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture. I'm really trying not to overreact to the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days since the crash where you felt unusually upset, scared, or emotional, even when nothing else was happening to cause it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I haven't really had any days like that. I'm doing okay, really!\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or interest in things you usually enjoy since the crash, like feeling more numb or detached?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I've felt numb or anything like that. I just don't want anyone to worry about me, so I make sure to keep a smile on and stay involved. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keeping busy and not letting yourself get down?
